<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 alluring</td><td>/əˈlʊrɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>诱人的，有魅力的</td><td>an alluring smile<br>迷人的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The alluring aroma of fresh bread drew us into the bakery.  新鲜面包诱人的香气把我们引进了面包店。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 captivating</td><td>/ˈkæptɪˌveɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，令人着迷的</td><td>a captivating performance<br>令人着迷的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her captivating voice held the entire audience in complete silence.  她迷人的歌声让全场观众鸦雀无声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 charismatic</td><td>/ˌkærɪzˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有魅力的，有感召力的</td><td>a charismatic leader<br>有感召力的领袖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The charismatic speaker convinced thousands to join his new movement.  这位有魅力的演讲者说服数千人加入他的新运动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 charming</td><td>/ˈtʃɑːrmɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，可爱的</td><td>a charming little village<br>迷人的小村庄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent the afternoon in a charming village by the lake.  我们在湖边一个迷人的村庄度过了下午。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enchanting</td><td>/ɪnˈtʃæntɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，醉人的</td><td>an enchanting melody<br>醉人的旋律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The garden looked enchanting under the soft light of lanterns.  花园在灯笼柔和的光线下显得十分迷人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 engaging</td><td>/ɪnˈɡeɪdʒɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有吸引力的，讨人喜欢的</td><td>an engaging personality<br>讨人喜欢的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His engaging manner made strangers feel comfortable within minutes.  他讨人喜欢的举止让陌生人几分钟内就放松下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 entrancing</td><td>/ɪnˈtrænsɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人入迷的，使人陶醉的</td><td>an entrancing dance<br>令人入迷的舞蹈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dancer's entrancing movements kept every eye fixed on the stage.  舞者令人入迷的动作让所有目光都盯着舞台。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 luring</td><td>/ˈlʊrɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引诱的，诱惑的</td><td>the luring promise of wealth<br>财富的诱人承诺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Advertisers use luring images to make ordinary products seem exciting.  广告商用诱人的图片让普通产品显得令人兴奋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 seductive</td><td>/sɪˈdʌktɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>诱人的，有诱惑力的</td><td>a seductive argument<br>诱人的论点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The idea of easy money is seductive but usually dangerous.  轻松赚钱的想法很诱人，但通常很危险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repellent</td><td>/rɪˈpelənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>令人厌恶的；驱虫剂</td><td>a repellent odor<br>令人厌恶的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I found his arrogant remarks utterly repellent and left early.  我觉得他傲慢的言论令人极其厌恶，于是提前离开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repelling</td><td>/rəˈpelɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人反感的，令人厌恶的</td><td>a repelling appearance<br>令人反感的外表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swamp gave off a repelling smell of rotting plants.  沼泽散发出腐烂植物令人厌恶的气味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repugnant</td><td>/rɪˈpʌɡnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的</td><td>morally repugnant behavior<br>道德上可憎的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most readers found the cruelty of that character morally repugnant and unforgivable.  多数读者觉得那个角色的残忍在道德上令人厌恶，不可宽恕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repulsive</td><td>/rɪˈpʌlsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，恶心的</td><td>a repulsive sight<br>令人恶心的景象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rotting fish on the beach was absolutely repulsive.  沙滩上腐烂的鱼令人极其恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unalluring</del></td><td>/ˌʌnəˈlʊrɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无吸引力的，不诱人的</td><td>an unalluring offer<br>毫无吸引力的提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cold, unalluring meal sat untouched on the plastic tray.  那份冰冷、毫无吸引力的饭菜在塑料托盘上没人动过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>applause</b></td><td>/əˈplɔːz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>鼓掌；认可，赞许</td><td>a round of applause<br>一阵掌声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her speech was greeted with long and warm applause.  她的演讲赢得了长时间热烈的掌声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 acclamation</td><td>/ˌækləˈmeɪʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>欢呼，喝彩</td><td>elected by acclamation<br>以欢呼方式选出</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new chairman was chosen by acclamation without a formal vote.  新主席在没有正式投票的情况下以欢呼方式选出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cheer</td><td>/ˈtʃɪr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>欢呼，喝彩；使高兴</td><td>a loud cheer<br>一阵响亮的欢呼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A loud cheer rose from the crowd when the goal was scored.  进球时人群中响起一阵响亮的欢呼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cheering</td><td>/ˈtʃɪrɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>欢呼的；令人振奋的</td><td>the cheering crowd<br>欢呼的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cheering fans waited outside the stadium for two hours.  欢呼的球迷在体育场外等了两个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ovation</td><td>/oʊˈveɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>热烈鼓掌，喝彩</td><td>a standing ovation<br>全体起立鼓掌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soprano received a standing ovation after her final aria.  女高音唱完最后一首咏叹调后获得全场起立鼓掌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 plaudit</del></td><td>/ˈplɔːdɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>赞美，喝彩</td><td>win critical plaudits<br>赢得评论界赞誉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her debut novel earned plaudits from critics across the country.  她的处女作小说赢得全国评论家的赞誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rave</td><td>/reɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>狂热赞扬；（发疯般地）怒吼</td><td>rave about the film<br>对影片赞不绝口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics raved about the film for weeks after its quiet premiere.  影片低调首映后，评论家连续数周对它赞不绝口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hissing</td><td>/ˈhɪsɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>嘘声，喝倒彩；发嘶嘶声的</td><td>hissing from the audience<br>观众的嘘声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speaker continued despite loud hissing from the back rows.  尽管后排传来响亮的嘘声，演讲者仍继续讲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 booing</td><td>/ˈbuːɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>喝倒彩，起哄</td><td>loud booing from fans<br>球迷响亮的喝倒彩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team left the field to booing from angry supporters.  球队在愤怒球迷的喝倒彩声中离场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>apposite</b></td><td>/ˈæpəzɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>相关的，合适的</td><td>an apposite comment<br>恰当的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made one apposite comment that settled the whole argument.  她一句恰当的评论就平息了整场争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 applicable</td><td>/ˈæplɪkəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可用的，可行的；相关的，有关的</td><td>applicable to all students<br>适用于所有学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This rule is applicable to all students regardless of their year.  这条规定适用于所有学生，不论他们是哪个年级。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apropos</td><td>/ˌæprəˈpoʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、介词</td><td>相关的；关于，有关</td><td>apropos of nothing<br>毫无来由地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Apropos of nothing, he asked whether I had ever been to Rome.  他毫无来由地问我是否去过罗马。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 germane</td><td>/dʒɜːrˈmeɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有关的，适当的</td><td>germane to the discussion<br>与讨论相关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her question was germane to the discussion and changed our plan.  她的问题与讨论密切相关，改变了我们的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pointed</td><td>/ˈpɔɪntɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖锐的，一针见血的</td><td>a pointed question<br>尖锐的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She asked a pointed question that the minister could not answer.  她提出了一个部长无法回答的尖锐问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relative</td><td>/ˈrelətɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>相关的；相对的；亲属</td><td>relative to the topic<br>与该话题相关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Comfort is relative and depends largely on what you expect.  舒适是相对的，很大程度上取决于你的期望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relevant</td><td>/ˈreləvənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有关系的，重要的</td><td>relevant to the case<br>与本案有关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge ruled that the old letters were relevant to the case.  法官裁定那些旧信件与本案有关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extraneous</td><td>/ɪkˈstreɪniəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不重要的</td><td>extraneous information<br>无关的信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please delete all extraneous details from the summary.  请把摘要中所有无关的细节删掉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 irrelevant</td><td>/ˌɪˈreləvənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不相干的</td><td>an irrelevant detail<br>无关的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer objected because the question was completely irrelevant here.  律师提出反对，因为这个问题在此完全无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impertinent</td><td>/ɪmˈpɜːrtnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不相关的；无礼的，鲁莽的</td><td>an impertinent remark<br>无礼的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer objected that the question was impertinent to the charges.  律师反对说，这个问题与指控无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 irrelative</del></td><td>/ɪˈrɛlətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不相干的</td><td>irrelative to the issue<br>与该问题无关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed the data as irrelative to his central argument.  他认为这些数据与他的核心论点无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pointless</td><td>/ˈpɔɪntləs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无意义的，徒劳的</td><td>a pointless argument<br>毫无意义的争论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Arguing with him about politics is completely pointless and exhausting.  和他争论政治完全没有意义，而且很累。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>appreciable</b></td><td>/əˈpriːʃəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可感知的，明显的</td><td>an appreciable difference<br>明显的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is no appreciable difference between these two brands of paint.  这两个牌子的油漆之间没有明显的差别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 apprehensible</del></td><td>/ˌæprɪˈhensəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可理解的</td><td>readily apprehensible ideas<br>易于理解的观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His argument is barely apprehensible to readers without training.  他的论证对没有受过训练的读者几乎难以理解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perceptible</td><td>/pərˈseptəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可察觉的</td><td>a barely perceptible change<br>几乎察觉不到的变化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was a barely perceptible change in her tone of voice.  她的语调有一丝几乎察觉不到的变化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 detectable</td><td>/dɪˈtektəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可检测到的，可察觉的</td><td>detectable levels of lead<br>可检测到的铅含量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new sensor finds detectable traces of gas within seconds.  新传感器几秒内就能发现可检测到的气体痕迹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discernible</td><td>/dɪˈsɜːrnəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可辨别的，可看出的</td><td>no discernible pattern<br>看不出规律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was no discernible improvement after three weeks of treatment.  治疗三周后没有可察觉的改善。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 palpable</td><td>/ˈpælpəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，易觉察的</td><td>a palpable sense of relief<br>明显的宽慰感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A palpable tension filled the room as the verdict was read aloud.  宣读判决时，房间里弥漫着明显的紧张气氛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distinguishable</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋɡwɪʃəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可区分的，可辨别的</td><td>clearly distinguishable from others<br>与其他明显可区分</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The twins are barely distinguishable except for a small scar.  这对双胞胎除了一道小疤几乎无法区分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sensible</td><td>/ˈsensəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，合理的</td><td>a sensible choice<br>明智的选择</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wearing boots was a sensible choice in that mud.  在那种泥地里穿靴子是明智的选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impalpable</td><td>/ɪmˈpælpəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以察觉的，无形的</td><td>an impalpable difference<br>难以察觉的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A faint, impalpable tension hung over the negotiating table.  谈判桌上笼罩着一种难以察觉的紧张气氛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 imperceptible</td><td>/ˌɪmpərˈseptɪbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>察觉不到的，极微小的</td><td>an imperceptible shift<br>察觉不到的变化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The change in temperature was almost imperceptible to the guests.  客人几乎察觉不到温度的变化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 inappreciable</del></td><td>/ˌɪnəˈpriʃəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>微不足道的，感觉不到的</td><td>an inappreciable amount<br>微不足道的量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The risk from such a tiny dose is inappreciable.  如此微小剂量带来的风险微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indistinguishable</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈstɪŋɡwɪʃəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法区分的，难以辨别的</td><td>indistinguishable from the original<br>与原件无法区分</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The copy was almost indistinguishable from the original painting.  这份复制品与原画几乎无法区分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 insensible</td><td>/ɪnˈsensəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无知觉的，昏迷的</td><td>insensible to pain<br>感觉不到疼痛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The blow left him insensible for several minutes.  这一击使他几分钟内失去了知觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 undetectable</td><td>/ˌʌndɪˈtektəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法检测到的，察觉不到的</td><td>undetectable by radar<br>雷达无法探测到</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The virus remained undetectable in blood tests for several weeks.  这种病毒在血液检测中数周都无法检出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 4

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>apprehension</b></td><td>/ˌæprɪˈhenʃn/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>忧虑，担心；逮捕</td><td>a sense of apprehension<br>忧虑感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She waited for the results with growing apprehension.  她越来越忧虑地等待结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dread</td><td>/dred/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恐惧</td><td>fill him with dread<br>使他充满恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thought of another interview filled him with dread all weekend.  一想到还要再面试一次，他整个周末都充满恐惧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foreboding</td><td>/fɔːrˈboʊdɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>不祥的；不祥的预感</td><td>a sense of foreboding<br>不祥的预感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She felt a strange foreboding as the sky darkened.  天色转暗时她有种奇怪的不祥预感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misgiving</td><td>/ˌmɪsˈɡɪvɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>担忧，疑虑</td><td>have serious misgivings<br>深感疑虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had serious misgivings about signing the contract without a lawyer present.  她对在没有律师在场的情况下签合同深感疑虑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 anxiousness</td><td>/ˈæŋkʃəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>焦虑，不安；忧虑</td><td>a sense of anxiousness<br>一种不安感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A faint anxiousness crept over her as the interview time drew near.  面试时间临近时，一丝不安悄悄涌上她心头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unease</td><td>/ʌnˈiːz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不安，忧虑</td><td>a feeling of unease<br>不安的感觉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A feeling of unease settled over the village after the warning.  警报之后，一种不安感笼罩了村庄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uneasiness</td><td>/ʌnˈiːzinəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>不安；忐忑；忧虑</td><td>a sense of uneasiness<br>一种不安感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A vague uneasiness settled over the crowd as the storm clouds gathered above them.  当暴风云在头顶聚集时，人群中弥漫着一种莫名的不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 worry</td><td>/ˈwɜːri/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>担忧，忧虑</td><td>a constant worry<br>长久的忧虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Money was a constant worry during their first year abroad.  在国外的第一年里，钱是他们持续的忧虑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 understanding</td><td>/ˌʌndərˈstændɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>理解；领悟；谅解</td><td>a thorough understanding<br>透彻的理解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A thorough understanding of the rules is essential before you begin playing the game.  在开始玩这个游戏之前，对规则有透彻的理解是必不可少的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 comprehension</td><td>/ˌkɑːmpriˈhenʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>理解，领悟；理解力</td><td>reading comprehension<br>阅读理解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scale of the disaster was almost beyond human comprehension at first.  灾难的规模起初几乎超出了人类的理解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 composure</td><td>/kəmˈpoʊʒər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>镇静，沉着</td><td>regain one's composure<br>重新恢复镇静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He lost his composure only once, when the verdict was read.  只有在宣读判决时，他才失了一次镇静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 equanimity</td><td>/ˌiːkwəˈnɪmɪti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>平静，镇定</td><td>with equanimity<br>以平静的心态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She accepted the bad news with surprising equanimity.  她以出人意料的平静接受了这个坏消息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 incomprehension</td><td>/ˌɪnkɑːmprɪˈhenʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不理解，无法领悟</td><td>a look of incomprehension<br>困惑不解的表情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stared at the equations with total incomprehension and quietly closed the textbook.  他一脸茫然地盯着方程式，悄悄合上了教科书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>apprise</b></td><td>/əˈpraɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>通知，告知</td><td>apprise him of the risks<br>告知他风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please apprise him of the risks before he signs the contract.  请在他签合同之前告知他风险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inform</td><td>/ɪnˈfɔːrm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>通知，告知；举报</td><td>inform the police immediately<br>立即通知警方</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please inform me as soon as the results become available.  结果一出来请立刻通知我。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 acquaint</td><td>/əˈkweɪnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使熟悉，使了解</td><td>acquaint yourself with the rules<br>熟悉这些规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New employees must acquaint themselves with the safety procedures first.  新员工必须先熟悉安全流程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 make known to</td><td>/meɪk ˈnoʊn tu/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>告知，向…公开</td><td>make known to all<br>向所有人公开</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The decision was made known to the staff on Monday.  这个决定于周一告知全体员工。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 withhold information</td><td>/wɪðˈhoʊld ˌɪnfərˈmeɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>隐瞒信息，不予告知</td><td>withhold information from investigators<br>向调查人员隐瞒信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company was fined for withholding information from the regulators.  公司因向监管机构隐瞒信息而被罚款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>approbation</b></td><td>/ˌæprəˈbeɪʃn/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>赞许，认可，同意</td><td>win official approbation<br>获得官方认可</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plan finally won the approbation of the whole city council.  该计划最终获得了整个市议会的同意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 approval</td><td>/əˈpruːvəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>批准，认可；赞成</td><td>seek her approval<br>征求她的同意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plan needs approval from the board before work begins.  该计划开工前需要董事会批准。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 favor</td><td>/ˈfeɪvər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>赞同，好评；恩惠</td><td>win public favor<br>赢得公众好评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The proposal quickly won favor among the younger members.  这项提议很快赢得年轻成员的支持。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disapproval</td><td>/dɪsəˈpruːvəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>不赞成，反对</td><td>a look of disapproval<br>不赞成的表情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her father showed clear disapproval of the hasty marriage.  她父亲明确表示不赞成这桩仓促的婚事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disapprobation</td><td>/ˌdɪsæprəˈbeɪʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不赞成，非难</td><td>public disapprobation<br>公众的非难</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His remarks drew strong disapprobation from senior colleagues.  他的言论招致资深同事的强烈非难。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disfavor</td><td>/dɪsˈfeɪvər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不赞成；失宠</td><td>fall into disfavor<br>失宠，不受待见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old method gradually fell into disfavor among researchers.  这种旧方法逐渐不受研究者青睐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>appropriate</b></td><td>/əˈproʊprieɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>私自挪用；适当的</td><td>appropriate public funds<br>挪用公款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Officials were accused of appropriating public money for private use.  官员们被指控将公款挪为私用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 purloin</del></td><td>/pɜːrˈlɔɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>偷窃</td><td>purloin a secret document<br>偷走一份密件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clerk managed to purloin a secret document from the locked cabinet.  那名职员设法从上锁的柜子里偷走了一份密件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pirate</td><td>/ˈpaɪrət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>盗版，盗用</td><td>pirate a software program<br>盗版软件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company sued several websites for pirating its accounting software.  该公司起诉了几家盗版其会计软件的网站。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 embezzle</td><td>/ɪmˈbezl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>盗用，挪用（公款）</td><td>embezzle company funds<br>挪用公司资金</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The accountant embezzled company funds for nearly six years without detection.  这名会计挪用公司资金近六年而未被发现。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 peculate</del></td><td>/ˈpɛkjəleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>挪用公款，盗用</td><td>peculate public money<br>盗用公款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treasurer was jailed for peculating funds over many years.  财务主管因多年盗用公款而入狱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 usurp</td><td>/juːˈzɜːrp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>篡夺，篡位</td><td>usurp the throne<br>篡夺王位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general usurped power after the president fled.  总统逃走后，将军篡夺了权力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apt</td><td>/æpt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恰当的，合适的；聪明的</td><td>an apt description<br>恰当的描述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That is an apt description of how the meeting actually went.  那是对会议实际情况的一个恰当描述。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 becoming</td><td>/bɪˈkʌmɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>合适的，得体的</td><td>a becoming dress<br>得体的裙子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her modest reply was becoming in such a formal setting.  在如此正式的场合，她谦逊的回答很得体。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 felicitous</td><td>/fɪˈlɪsətəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恰当的，贴切的</td><td>a felicitous phrase<br>贴切的措辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The title is a felicitous choice for such a book.  这个书名对这样一本书来说是贴切的选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fitting</td><td>/ˈfɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>合适的，恰当的</td><td>a fitting tribute<br>恰当的致敬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The quiet ceremony was a fitting end to his career.  这场安静的仪式为他的职业生涯做了恰当的收尾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 proper</td><td>/ˈprɑːpər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>适当的，正确的</td><td>the proper procedure<br>正确的程序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please store the samples in the proper containers.  请把样本存放在合适的容器里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 meet</td><td>/miːt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>合适的</td><td>a meet response<br>合适的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A public apology seemed the only meet response to such a serious blunder.  公开道歉似乎是对如此严重失误唯一合适的回应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 suitable</td><td>/ˈsuːtəbəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>合适的，适宜的</td><td>suitable for beginners<br>适合初学者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This film is not suitable for very young children.  这部影片不适合幼儿观看。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apposite</td><td>/ˈæpəzɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>相关的，合适的</td><td>an apposite comment<br>恰当的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made one apposite comment that settled the whole argument.  她一句恰当的评论就平息了整场争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apropos</td><td>/ˌæprəˈpoʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、介词</td><td>相关的；关于，有关</td><td>apropos of nothing<br>毫无来由地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Apropos of nothing, he asked whether I had ever been to Rome.  他毫无来由地问我是否去过罗马。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 improper</td><td>/ˌɪmˈprɑːpər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不适当的，不合礼节的</td><td>improper use of funds<br>资金使用不当</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was dismissed for improper conduct toward a colleague.  他因对同事行为不当而被解雇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 inapposite</del></td><td>/ɪnˈæpəzɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不恰当的，不相关的</td><td>an inapposite comparison<br>不恰当的比较</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The comparison to wartime rationing seems inapposite today.  把它比作战时配给在今天显得不恰当。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inappropriate</td><td>/ˌɪnəˈproʊpriət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不适当的，不合适的</td><td>inappropriate behavior at work<br>工作中的不当行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wearing shorts to the interview was clearly inappropriate.  穿短裤去面试显然不合适。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inapt</td><td>/ɪnˈæpt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不恰当的；不熟练的</td><td>an inapt analogy<br>不恰当的类比</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His inapt remarks embarrassed everyone at the dinner table.  他不得体的言论让餐桌上的人都很尴尬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unmeet</del></td><td>/ʌnˈmit/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不合适的，不相称的</td><td>unmeet for the occasion<br>与场合不相称</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Such loud laughter seemed unmeet at a solemn funeral.  在庄严的葬礼上这样大笑显得不合适。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unseemly</td><td>/ʌnˈsiːmli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不得体的，不适宜的</td><td>unseemly haste<br>失礼的匆忙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their unseemly quarrel spoiled the whole wedding reception.  他们不得体的争吵毁了整场婚宴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unsuitable</td><td>/ʌnˈsuːtəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不合适的，不适宜的</td><td>unsuitable for children<br>不适合儿童</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soil here is completely unsuitable for growing rice.  这里的土壤完全不适合种稻子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>apropos</b></td><td>/ˌæprəˈpoʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、介词</td><td>相关的；关于，有关</td><td>apropos of nothing<br>毫无来由地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Apropos of nothing, he asked whether I had ever been to Rome.  他毫无来由地问我是否去过罗马。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 applicable</td><td>/ˈæplɪkəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可用的，可行的；相关的，有关的</td><td>applicable to all students<br>适用于所有学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This rule is applicable to all students regardless of their year.  这条规定适用于所有学生，不论他们是哪个年级。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apposite</td><td>/ˈæpəzɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>相关的，合适的</td><td>an apposite comment<br>恰当的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made one apposite comment that settled the whole argument.  她一句恰当的评论就平息了整场争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 germane</td><td>/dʒɜːrˈmeɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有关的，适当的</td><td>germane to the discussion<br>与讨论相关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her question was germane to the discussion and changed our plan.  她的问题与讨论密切相关，改变了我们的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pointed</td><td>/ˈpɔɪntɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖锐的，一针见血的</td><td>a pointed question<br>尖锐的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She asked a pointed question that the minister could not answer.  她提出了一个部长无法回答的尖锐问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relative</td><td>/ˈrelətɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>相关的；相对的；亲属</td><td>relative to the topic<br>与该话题相关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Comfort is relative and depends largely on what you expect.  舒适是相对的，很大程度上取决于你的期望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relevant</td><td>/ˈreləvənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有关系的，重要的</td><td>relevant to the case<br>与本案有关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge ruled that the old letters were relevant to the case.  法官裁定那些旧信件与本案有关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 specting</del></td><td>/ˈspɛktɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>介词</td><td>关于，涉及</td><td>specting the whole matter<br>关于整件事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Specting the new rules, nobody seemed to understand them.  关于新规定，似乎没人真正理解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 touching</td><td>/ˈtʌtʃɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>介词、形容词</td><td>关于，涉及；动人的</td><td>touching the question of cost<br>关于成本问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The letter said little touching the terms of payment.  信中几乎没有提及付款条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 toward</td><td>/təˈwɔːrd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>介词</td><td>关于，对于；朝向</td><td>his attitude toward work<br>他对工作的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her policy toward small businesses has changed a great deal.  她对小企业的政策已经改变了很多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extraneous</td><td>/ɪkˈstreɪniəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不重要的</td><td>extraneous information<br>无关的信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please delete all extraneous details from the summary.  请把摘要中所有无关的细节删掉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 irrelevant</td><td>/ˌɪˈreləvənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不相干的</td><td>an irrelevant detail<br>无关的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer objected because the question was completely irrelevant here.  律师提出反对，因为这个问题在此完全无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impertinent</td><td>/ɪmˈpɜːrtnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不相关的；无礼的，鲁莽的</td><td>an impertinent remark<br>无礼的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer objected that the question was impertinent to the charges.  律师反对说，这个问题与指控无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 irrelative</del></td><td>/ɪˈrɛlətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不相干的</td><td>irrelative to the issue<br>与该问题无关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed the data as irrelative to his central argument.  他认为这些数据与他的核心论点无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pointless</td><td>/ˈpɔɪntləs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无意义的，徒劳的</td><td>a pointless argument<br>毫无意义的争论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Arguing with him about politics is completely pointless and exhausting.  和他争论政治完全没有意义，而且很累。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>apt</b></td><td>/æpt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恰当的，合适的；聪明的</td><td>an apt description<br>恰当的描述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That is an apt description of how the meeting actually went.  那是对会议实际情况的一个恰当描述。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apropos</td><td>/ˌæprəˈpoʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、介词</td><td>相关的；关于，有关</td><td>apropos of nothing<br>毫无来由地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Apropos of nothing, he asked whether I had ever been to Rome.  他毫无来由地问我是否去过罗马。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 germane</td><td>/dʒɜːrˈmeɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有关的，适当的</td><td>germane to the discussion<br>与讨论相关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her question was germane to the discussion and changed our plan.  她的问题与讨论密切相关，改变了我们的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relative</td><td>/ˈrelətɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>相关的；相对的；亲属</td><td>relative to the topic<br>与该话题相关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Comfort is relative and depends largely on what you expect.  舒适是相对的，很大程度上取决于你的期望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relevant</td><td>/ˈreləvənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有关系的，重要的</td><td>relevant to the case<br>与本案有关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge ruled that the old letters were relevant to the case.  法官裁定那些旧信件与本案有关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appropriate</td><td>/əˈproʊprieɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>私自挪用；适当的</td><td>appropriate public funds<br>挪用公款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Officials were accused of appropriating public money for private use.  官员们被指控将公款挪为私用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brilliant</td><td>/ˈbrɪljənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪颖的；辉煌的</td><td>a brilliant student<br>才华出众的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was a brilliant mathematician long before she turned twenty.  她还没到二十岁就已是出色的数学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clever</td><td>/ˈklevər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的，机敏的</td><td>a clever solution<br>巧妙的解决办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That was a clever way to avoid the extra cost.  那是避免额外成本的聪明办法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quick-witted</td><td>/ˌkwɪk ˈwɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>机智的，反应快的</td><td>a quick-witted reply<br>机智的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The quick-witted intern saved the presentation with one remark.  机智的实习生用一句话救了那场演示。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ready-witted</td><td>/ˌrɛdi ˈwɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>机敏的，才思敏捷的</td><td>a ready-witted debater<br>才思敏捷的辩手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a ready-witted host could handle such rude questions.  只有才思敏捷的主持人才能应对这种无礼的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 smart</td><td>/ˈsmɑːrt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的；时髦的</td><td>a smart move<br>明智之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Buying the tickets early was a very smart decision.  提前买票是个非常明智的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extraneous</td><td>/ɪkˈstreɪniəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不重要的</td><td>extraneous information<br>无关的信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please delete all extraneous details from the summary.  请把摘要中所有无关的细节删掉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 irrelevant</td><td>/ˌɪˈreləvənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不相干的</td><td>an irrelevant detail<br>无关的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer objected because the question was completely irrelevant here.  律师提出反对，因为这个问题在此完全无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impertinent</td><td>/ɪmˈpɜːrtnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不相关的；无礼的，鲁莽的</td><td>an impertinent remark<br>无礼的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer objected that the question was impertinent to the charges.  律师反对说，这个问题与指控无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 irrelative</del></td><td>/ɪˈrɛlətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不相干的</td><td>irrelative to the issue<br>与该问题无关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed the data as irrelative to his central argument.  他认为这些数据与他的核心论点无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 foolish</td><td>/ˈfuːlɪʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，傻的</td><td>a foolish mistake<br>愚蠢的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was foolish of him to ignore the warning signs.  他忽视警示标志实在愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dull</td><td>/dʌl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迟钝的；乏味的</td><td>a dull student<br>迟钝的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture was so dull that half the class slept.  讲座乏味得让一半学生睡着了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dumb</td><td>/dʌm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的；不会说话的</td><td>a dumb question<br>愚蠢的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Asking that in public was a pretty dumb idea.  在公开场合问那个是个相当愚蠢的想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stupid</td><td>/ˈstuːpəd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，笨的</td><td>a stupid mistake<br>愚蠢的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I felt stupid after mixing up their names again.  又把他们的名字弄混后我觉得自己很笨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unintelligent</td><td>/ˌʌnɪnˈtɛlɪdʒənt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不聪明的，愚笨的</td><td>an unintelligent remark<br>愚笨的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The character is lazy but by no means unintelligent.  这个角色懒惰，但绝不愚笨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>archaic</b></td><td>/ɑːrˈkeɪɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>过时的，久远的</td><td>archaic legal language<br>古旧的法律用语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract still uses archaic terms that nobody understands.  这份合同仍在使用无人理解的古旧术语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 antiquated</td><td>/ˈæntɪkweɪtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>古老的，过时的</td><td>an antiquated system<br>过时的系统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The office still runs on an antiquated system built in the eighties.  办公室仍在使用八十年代建成的过时系统。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outdated</td><td>/ˈaʊtˌdeɪtɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过时的，陈旧的</td><td>outdated equipment<br>过时的设备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their outdated software could not handle the new files.  他们过时的软件无法处理新文件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outmoded</td><td>/ˌaʊtˈmoʊdɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过时的，废弃的</td><td>outmoded ideas<br>过时的观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The factory still relies on outmoded machinery from the nineteen sixties.  这家工厂仍在使用二十世纪六十年代的过时机器。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prehistoric</td><td>/ˌpriːhɪˈstɔːrɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>史前的；极古老的</td><td>prehistoric cave paintings<br>史前洞穴壁画</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Archaeologists found prehistoric tools beside the dried riverbed.  考古学家在干涸的河床旁发现了史前工具。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 superannuated</td><td>/ˌsupərˈænjueɪtɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过时的；退休的</td><td>a superannuated theory<br>过时的理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The factory still runs on superannuated machinery from the 1960s.  这家工厂仍在使用上世纪六十年代的老旧机器。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fashionable</td><td>/ˈfæʃənəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>时髦的，流行的</td><td>a fashionable neighborhood<br>时尚的街区</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long coats became fashionable again after that film came out.  那部电影上映后长外套又流行起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modern diction</td><td>/ˈmɑdərn ˈdɪkʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>现代措辞，当代用语</td><td>clear modern diction<br>清晰的现代措辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The translator replaced the old verses with modern diction.  译者用现代措辞替换了那些旧诗句。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>archetype</b></td><td>/ˈɑːrkɪtaɪp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>原型，典型</td><td>the archetype of a hero<br>英雄的典型</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sherlock Holmes remains the archetype of the brilliant private detective.  夏洛克·福尔摩斯仍是天才私家侦探的原型。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ideal</td><td>/aɪˈdiːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>理想，典范；理想的</td><td>an ideal solution<br>理想的解决办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She remains the ideal of quiet courage for many students.  她仍是许多学生心中沉静勇气的典范。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 a perfect example</td><td>/ə ˈpɜrfɪkt ɪɡˈzæmpəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>完美的范例，典型</td><td>a perfect example of courage<br>勇气的完美范例</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This building is a perfect example of early modern design.  这座建筑是早期现代设计的完美范例。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quintessence</td><td>/kwɪnˈtesəns/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>精髓，典范</td><td>the quintessence of style<br>风格的精髓</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her writing is the quintessence of restraint and precision.  她的文字是克制与精确的精髓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ardor</b></td><td>/ˈɑːrdər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>狂热</td><td>revolutionary ardor<br>革命热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young recruits sang with revolutionary ardor all the way to the front.  年轻的新兵一路满怀革命热情地高唱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 avidity</td><td>/əˈvɪdɪti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>热切，渴望</td><td>read with avidity<br>如饥似渴地阅读</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He collected old maps with an avidity that surprised us.  他收集旧地图的热切劲头让我们吃惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 zeal</td><td>/ziːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>热心，热诚</td><td>religious zeal<br>宗教热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took up the campaign with the zeal of a true believer.  她以真正信徒的热诚投入了这场运动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fervor</td><td>/ˈfɜːrvər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>热情，热烈</td><td>patriotic fervor<br>爱国热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd sang the anthem with surprising fervor.  人群以惊人的热情唱起国歌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 fervency</del></td><td>/ˈfɜrvənsi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>热诚，炽热</td><td>speak with fervency<br>热诚地讲话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She defended her theory with a fervency few expected.  她以少有人预料的热诚为自己的理论辩护。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 fervidness</del></td><td>/ˈfɜrvɪdnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>热烈，狂热</td><td>the fervidness of youth<br>青春的热烈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fervidness of his belief unsettled his calmer colleagues.  他信念的狂热让较为冷静的同事不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 passion</td><td>/ˈpæʃən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>热情，激情</td><td>a passion for music<br>对音乐的热爱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He talks about old trains with genuine passion.  他谈起老火车时充满真挚的热情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 passionateness</del></td><td>/ˈpæʃənətnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>热情，激烈</td><td>the passionateness of her plea<br>她请求的热切</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The passionateness of the debate startled the visiting scholars.  辩论的激烈程度让来访学者感到惊讶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vehemence</td><td>/ˈviːəməns/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>激烈，强烈</td><td>argue with vehemence<br>激烈地争辩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The vehemence of his denial surprised everyone in the room.  他否认的激烈程度让屋里所有人吃惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 apathy</td><td>/ˈæpəθi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>缺乏兴趣，不关心</td><td>public apathy toward voting<br>公众对投票的冷漠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Widespread apathy among young voters worried the campaign organizers.  年轻选民中普遍的冷漠让竞选组织者担忧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 torpor</td><td>/ˈtɔːrpər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>缺乏兴趣；（肉体或精神上的）迟钝，懒散</td><td>sink into torpor<br>陷入迟钝麻木</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The heat sank the whole village into a heavy afternoon torpor.  暑热让整个村子陷入沉沉的午后麻木。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 impassivity</del></td><td>/ˌɪmpæˈsɪvɪti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冷漠，无动于衷</td><td>an air of impassivity<br>冷漠的神情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness listened with total impassivity to the verdict.  证人完全无动于衷地听着判决。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 insensitivity</td><td>/ˌɪnsensəˈtɪvəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冷漠，麻木不仁</td><td>insensitivity to criticism<br>对批评的麻木</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His insensitivity to other people's feelings cost him friends.  他对别人感受的麻木让他失去了朋友。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>arduous</b></td><td>/ˈɑːrdʒuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以做到的，费劲的</td><td>an arduous journey<br>艰苦的旅程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The climb proved far more arduous than any of them expected.  这次攀登远比他们任何人预想的都费劲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grueling</td><td>/ˈɡruːəlɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>累人的，折磨人的</td><td>a grueling schedule<br>折磨人的日程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team endured a grueling schedule of twelve games in three weeks.  球队熬过了三周十二场比赛这种折磨人的赛程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 laborious</td><td>/ləˈbɔːriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>费力的，艰苦的；勤奋的</td><td>a laborious process<br>费力的过程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Copying the manuscript by hand proved a slow and laborious process.  手抄这份手稿是一个缓慢而费力的过程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 taxing</td><td>/ˈtæksɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>繁重的，费力的</td><td>a taxing task<br>繁重的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Caring for two infants proved a taxing task for the young couple.  照顾两个婴儿对这对年轻夫妻是繁重的任务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 onerous</td><td>/ˈɑːnərəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>繁重的，费力的</td><td>onerous responsibilities<br>繁重的责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Caring for the archive proved more onerous than the volunteers had expected.  照管这份档案比志愿者们预想的更费时费心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 burdensome</td><td>/ˈbɜːrdənsəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>繁重的，累人的</td><td>burdensome regulations<br>繁琐的规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new paperwork proved burdensome for small rural clinics.  新的文书工作对乡村小诊所来说很繁重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 easy</td><td>/ˈiːzi/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>容易的，不费力的</td><td>an easy question<br>简单的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The homework looked easy, but it took him three hours to finish.  作业看起来容易，可他花了三个小时才做完。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 simple</td><td>/ˈsɪmpəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简单的，容易的</td><td>a simple task<br>简单的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Follow these simple steps and the machine will restart.  按这些简单步骤操作，机器就会重启。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unchallenging</td><td>/ˌʌnˈtʃælɪndʒɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有挑战性的，轻松的</td><td>an unchallenging job<br>没有挑战的工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He quit the unchallenging position after only three months.  他只干了三个月就辞掉那份没有挑战性的工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 undemanding</td><td>/ˌʌndɪˈmændɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>要求不高的，轻松的</td><td>an undemanding course<br>要求不高的课程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She chose an undemanding role so she could rest more.  她选了个轻松的角色以便多休息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 facile</td><td>/ˈfæsl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>容易做到的；肤浅轻率的</td><td>a facile explanation<br>肤浅的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report offers a facile explanation for a very complex problem.  这份报告对一个非常复杂的问题给出了肤浅的解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 effortless</td><td>/ˈefərtləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻松的，不费力的</td><td>effortless grace<br>毫不费力的优雅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her swimming looked effortless even in the rough water.  即使在波涛中她的游泳也显得毫不费力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 5

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>anthropogenic</b></td><td>/ˌænθrəpəˈdʒenɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>人为的</td><td>anthropogenic climate change<br>人为气候变化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most scientists agree that anthropogenic emissions drive the current rise in global temperatures.  多数科学家认为人为排放推动了当前全球气温的上升。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 caused by human</td><td>/kɔːzd baɪ ˈhjuːmən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>由人类引起的，人为的</td><td>damage caused by human activity<br>人类活动造成的破坏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Much of the reef damage caused by human activity could still be reversed.  人类活动造成的珊瑚礁破坏中，很大一部分仍可挽回。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>arrest</b></td><td>/əˈrest/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>停止；逮捕</td><td>place him under arrest<br>将他逮捕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new drug can arrest the spread of the disease for years.  这种新药能在数年内阻止疾病扩散。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cease</td><td>/ˈsiːs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>停止，终止</td><td>cease all operations<br>停止一切运作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company will cease production at that plant in June.  公司将于六月停止该厂的生产。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cessation</td><td>/seˈseɪʃn/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>终止，暂停</td><td>a cessation of hostilities<br>停止敌对行动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both sides finally agreed to a cessation of hostilities.  双方最终同意停止敌对行动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 closure</td><td>/ˈkloʊʒər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>关闭，终止</td><td>the closure of the factory<br>工厂的关闭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The closure of the branch left thirty people jobless.  分店关闭让三十人失业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conclusion</td><td>/kənˈkluːʒən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>结束；结论</td><td>bring to a conclusion<br>使结束</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The trial reached its conclusion after eleven long weeks.  审判在漫长的十一周后结束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discontinuance</td><td>/ˌdɪskənˈtɪnjuəns/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>停止，中断</td><td>the discontinuance of the service<br>服务的停止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The discontinuance of the bus route angered local residents.  公交线路的停运惹恼了当地居民。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discontinuation</td><td>/ˌdɪskəntɪnjuˈeɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>中止，停止</td><td>the discontinuation of funding<br>资金的中止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors warned against sudden discontinuation of the medication.  医生警告不要突然停药。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ending</td><td>/ˈendɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>结局，结尾</td><td>a happy ending<br>幸福的结局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel has an ending that nobody sees coming.  这部小说的结局谁都想不到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 halt</td><td>/ˈhɔːlt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>停止，暂停</td><td>come to a halt<br>停下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy snow brought the whole city to a halt.  大雪使整座城市陷入停滞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stop</td><td>/ˈstɑːp/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>停止；停靠站</td><td>put a stop to<br>制止，终止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The referee stopped the match because of the sudden storm.  裁判因突来的暴风雨中止了比赛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 termination</td><td>/tərməˈneɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>终止，结束</td><td>the termination of the contract<br>合同的终止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Either side may request termination of the agreement early.  任何一方都可要求提前终止协议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 check</td><td>/tʃek/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使突然停止，阻止；检查，核对</td><td>check the spread of disease<br>阻止疾病蔓延</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Strict quarantine measures checked the spread of the disease.  严格的隔离措施阻止了疾病的蔓延。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 close</td><td>/kloʊz/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>关闭；结束</td><td>close the meeting<br>结束会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They closed the discussion before anyone could object.  他们在有人反对之前结束了讨论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conclude</td><td>/kənˈkluːd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>结束；得出结论</td><td>conclude the ceremony<br>结束仪式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge concluded that the evidence was too weak.  法官得出结论认为证据太薄弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discontinue</td><td>/dɪskənˈtɪnju/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>停止，中断</td><td>discontinue the product line<br>停产该产品线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The brand discontinued its cheapest model last year.  该品牌去年停产了最便宜的型号。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 end</td><td>/ˈend/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>结束；末端</td><td>end the war<br>结束战争</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strike ended after both sides agreed on wages.  双方就工资达成一致后罢工结束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 terminate</td><td>/ˈtɜːrməˌneɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>终止，结束</td><td>terminate the agreement<br>终止协议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The club terminated his membership without any explanation.  俱乐部未加解释就终止了他的会员资格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apprehend</td><td>/ˌæprɪˈhend/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>逮捕；理解</td><td>apprehend the suspect<br>逮捕嫌疑人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police apprehended the thief two streets from the shop.  警方在离商店两条街的地方逮住了小偷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enchant</td><td>/ɪnˈtʃænt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使入迷，使陶醉</td><td>be enchanted by the scenery<br>被风景迷住</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old fishing village enchanted every visitor who stayed there.  那个古老渔村令每位住过的游客陶醉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fascinate</td><td>/ˈfæsɪneɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强烈吸引，使入迷</td><td>fascinate young readers<br>使小读者着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stories about strange deep-sea creatures fascinate young readers all over the world.  关于奇异深海生物的故事让全世界的小读者着迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bedazzle</td><td>/bɪˈdæzəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使眼花缭乱，使着迷</td><td>bedazzle the audience<br>使观众目眩神迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The acrobats bedazzled the crowd with almost impossible leaps.  杂技演员用几乎不可能的腾跃让观众目眩神迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grip</td><td>/ˈɡrɪp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>吸引住；紧握</td><td>grip the reader's attention<br>抓住读者注意力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film gripped us from the very first scene.  这部电影从第一个镜头就抓住了我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hypnotize</td><td>/ˈhɪpnəˌtaɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使着迷；对…施催眠术</td><td>hypnotize the audience<br>使观众入迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dancer's slow movements seemed to hypnotize everyone watching.  舞者缓慢的动作似乎让所有观看者入迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mesmerize</td><td>/ˈmezməraɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使入迷，迷住</td><td>mesmerize the whole crowd<br>迷住全场人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The flickering firelight mesmerized the tired children and kept them quiet.  跳动的火光让疲倦的孩子们看得入迷，安静了下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 continuance</td><td>/kənˈtɪnjuəns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>继续，持续</td><td>the continuance of peace<br>和平的延续</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Everyone hoped for the continuance of the fragile ceasefire.  所有人都希望脆弱的停火能够持续。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 continuation</td><td>/kənˌtɪnjuˈeɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>继续，延续</td><td>the continuation of talks<br>会谈的继续</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The continuation of the project depends on further funding.  项目的继续取决于后续资金。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 discharge</td><td>/dɪsˈtʃɑːrdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>释放；解雇</td><td>discharge the prisoner<br>释放囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court discharged the prisoner immediately after the new evidence appeared.  新证据出现后，法庭立即释放了那名囚犯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>arresting</b></td><td>/əˈrestɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吸引人的</td><td>an arresting image<br>引人注目的画面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poster used an arresting image of a burning city.  海报用了一幅燃烧城市的醒目画面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 absorbing</td><td>/əbˈzɔːrbɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人入胜的，吸引人的</td><td>an absorbing novel<br>引人入胜的小说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I found her account of the voyage utterly absorbing.  我觉得她对航行的叙述极其引人入胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 engaging</td><td>/ɪnˈɡeɪdʒɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有吸引力的，讨人喜欢的</td><td>an engaging personality<br>讨人喜欢的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His engaging manner made strangers feel comfortable within minutes.  他讨人喜欢的举止让陌生人几分钟内就放松下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 engrossing</td><td>/ɪnˈɡroʊsɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人入胜的，令人专注的</td><td>an engrossing story<br>引人入胜的故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary was so engrossing that nobody left early.  这部纪录片如此引人入胜，没人提早离场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enthralling</td><td>/ɪnˈθrɔlɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人入迷的，极吸引人的</td><td>an enthralling performance<br>令人入迷的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her enthralling storytelling kept the children silent for an hour.  她迷人的讲述让孩子们安静了一个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fascinating</td><td>/ˈfæsəˌneɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，极有趣的</td><td>a fascinating discovery<br>引人入胜的发现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Insect behavior is a fascinating subject for young scientists.  昆虫行为对年轻科学家来说是极有趣的课题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gripping</td><td>/ˈɡrɪpɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>扣人心弦的，紧张的</td><td>a gripping thriller<br>扣人心弦的惊悚片</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The final chapter is gripping from start to finish.  最后一章从头到尾都扣人心弦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immersing</td><td>/ɪˈmɜrsɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人沉浸的，投入的</td><td>an immersing experience<br>令人沉浸的体验</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The game offers an immersing world full of hidden details.  这款游戏提供了充满隐藏细节的沉浸世界。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intriguing</td><td>/ˌɪnˈtriːɡɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人好奇的，有趣的</td><td>an intriguing question<br>引人好奇的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She raised an intriguing point that nobody had considered.  她提出了一个无人考虑过的有趣观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 riveting</td><td>/ˈrɪvɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吸引人的，极迷人的</td><td>a riveting account<br>引人入胜的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary offers a riveting account of life inside the mountain villages.  这部纪录片对山村生活的叙述引人入胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 boring</td><td>/ˈbɔːrɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无聊的，令人厌烦的</td><td>a boring lecture<br>无聊的讲座</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The meeting was so boring that I nearly fell asleep.  会议无聊得我差点睡着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 drab</td><td>/dræb/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调的，无聊的</td><td>drab gray walls<br>单调的灰墙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They painted over the drab gray walls with something warmer and brighter.  他们把单调的灰墙涂上了更暖更亮的颜色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tedious</td><td>/ˈtiːdiəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长乏味的</td><td>a tedious explanation<br>冗长乏味的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager gave a tedious explanation of the new filing system.  经理对新的归档系统作了冗长乏味的解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 monotonous</td><td>/məˈnɑːtənəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调的，无变化的</td><td>a monotonous voice<br>单调的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His monotonous voice made the afternoon lecture almost impossible to sit through.  他单调的声音让下午的讲座几乎难以听完。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 uninteresting</td><td>/ʌnˈɪntrəstɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无趣的，乏味的</td><td>an uninteresting report<br>乏味的报告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The food at the small hotel was cheap but completely uninteresting.  这家小旅馆的食物便宜，却毫无趣味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>arrhythmic</b></td><td>/əˈrɪðmɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不规律的</td><td>an arrhythmic heartbeat<br>不规律的心跳</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The patient's arrhythmic pulse worried the young nurse.  病人不规律的脉搏让年轻护士担心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 irregular</td><td>/ɪˈreɡjələr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不规则的，不平整的</td><td>an irregular shape<br>不规则的形状</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The path was made of irregular stones of many sizes.  这条小路由各种大小不规则的石头铺成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disorderly</td><td>/dɪˈsɔːrdərli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>混乱的，无秩序的</td><td>a disorderly crowd<br>混乱的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Books lay in disorderly piles across the whole floor.  书杂乱地堆在整个地板上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 regular</td><td>/ˈreɡjələr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>规律的，通常的</td><td>on a regular basis<br>定期地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He goes swimming on a regular basis every week.  他每周都定期去游泳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 orderly</td><td>/ˈɔːrdərli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有秩序的，整齐的</td><td>an orderly queue<br>整齐的队伍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The students left the hall in an orderly line.  学生们排成整齐的队伍离开礼堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>arrogance</b></td><td>/ˈærəɡəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>傲慢，自大</td><td>sheer arrogance<br>十足的傲慢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His arrogance made it impossible for colleagues to offer honest advice.  他的傲慢让同事们无法提出诚实的建议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assumption</td><td>/əˈsʌmpʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>自大，傲慢；假设</td><td>an air of assumption<br>自大的神气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His quiet assumption of authority annoyed the older members.  他不声不响地自居权威，惹恼了年长成员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 bumptiousness</del></td><td>/ˈbʌmpʃəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>自负，傲慢</td><td>youthful bumptiousness<br>年轻人的自负</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His bumptiousness made him unpopular with the entire team.  他的自负让整个团队都不喜欢他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 haughtiness</td><td>/ˈhɔtinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>傲慢，高傲</td><td>cold haughtiness<br>冷淡的高傲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The duchess treated the servants with unmistakable haughtiness.  公爵夫人对仆人明显地高傲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hauteur</td><td>/hɔːˈtɜːr/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>傲慢，自大</td><td>an air of hauteur<br>傲慢的神态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her hauteur offended the very people whose support she needed most.  她的傲慢恰恰得罪了她最需要支持的那些人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 imperiousness</del></td><td>/ɪmˈpɪriəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>专横，霸道</td><td>the imperiousness of power<br>权力带来的专横</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His imperiousness in meetings silenced every junior colleague.  他在会上的专横让每个年轻同事都不敢开口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 loftiness</del></td><td>/ˈlɔftinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>高傲，自大</td><td>intellectual loftiness<br>智识上的高傲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke with a loftiness that irritated the waiting reporters.  她说话时的高傲让等候的记者们不快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 peremptoriness</del></td><td>/pəˈrɛmptərinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>专横，独断</td><td>the peremptoriness of his order<br>他命令的专横</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The peremptoriness of the memo offended the whole department.  那份备忘录的专横语气得罪了整个部门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pomposity</td><td>/pɑːmˈpɑːsəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>自大，浮夸</td><td>academic pomposity<br>学术上的浮夸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech was ruined by pomposity and empty phrases.  演讲被浮夸和空话毁了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 pompousness</del></td><td>/ˈpɑːmpəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>自负，浮夸</td><td>unbearable pompousness<br>令人难忍的浮夸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His pompousness at dinner made the guests exchange glances.  他在晚餐上的浮夸让客人们交换眼神。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 presumptuousness</del></td><td>/prɪˈzʌmptʃuəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冒失，放肆自大</td><td>sheer presumptuousness<br>十足的放肆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Correcting his own mentor in front of the class showed remarkable presumptuousness.  在全班面前纠正自己的导师，显示出惊人的放肆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 superciliousness</del></td><td>/ˌsupərˈsɪliəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>傲慢，目中无人</td><td>chilly superciliousness<br>冷冰冰的傲慢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic's superciliousness annoyed even his most loyal readers.  这位评论家的傲慢连最忠实的读者都反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humility</td><td>/hjuˈmɪlɪti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>谦逊，谦卑</td><td>accept with humility<br>谦逊地接受</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He accepted the prize with genuine humility and short thanks.  他以真诚的谦逊和简短的致谢接受了奖项。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modesty</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəsti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>谦虚，谦逊</td><td>false modesty<br>假谦虚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her modesty about the discovery impressed the whole committee.  她对这一发现的谦逊让整个委员会印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humbleness</td><td>/ˈhʌmbəlnəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>谦卑，谦逊</td><td>quiet humbleness<br>沉静的谦卑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old monk's humbleness taught the students more than words.  老僧的谦卑教给学生的比言语更多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unassumingness</del></td><td>/ˌʌnəˈsumɪŋnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谦逊，不装架子</td><td>natural unassumingness<br>天然的谦逊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unassumingness made colleagues trust her almost immediately.  她的谦逊让同事几乎立刻就信任她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>articulate</b></td><td>/ɑːrˈtɪkjuleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>清晰地表达；表达清晰的</td><td>articulate her concerns<br>清晰表达她的顾虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She managed to articulate her concerns clearly at the board meeting.  她在董事会上清晰地表达了自己的顾虑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enunciate</td><td>/ɪˈnʌnsieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、及物动词</td><td>清晰地说；公开宣布，宣称</td><td>enunciate each word clearly<br>清晰吐出每个词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please enunciate each word clearly so the people at the back can hear.  请把每个词清晰地吐出，好让后排的人听清。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 eloquent</td><td>/ˈeləkwənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>雄辩的，有说服力的</td><td>an eloquent speech<br>雄辩的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His eloquent defense of the plan won several extra votes.  他对该计划雄辩的辩护多赢得了几张选票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fluent</td><td>/ˈfluːənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>表达流利的</td><td>fluent in three languages<br>精通三门语言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is fluent in three languages and translates for the court.  她精通三门语言，为法庭做翻译。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 silver-tongued</td><td>/ˌsɪlvər ˈtʌŋd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>口才好的，能言善辩的</td><td>a silver-tongued lawyer<br>能言善辩的律师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The silver-tongued salesman persuaded her to buy two cars.  能言善辩的销售员说服她买了两辆车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 well-spoken</td><td>/ˌwɛl ˈspoʊkən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谈吐文雅的，口齿清晰的</td><td>a well-spoken young man<br>谈吐文雅的青年</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The well-spoken candidate impressed everyone on the hiring panel.  谈吐文雅的候选人给招聘小组留下深刻印象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 murmur</td><td>/ˈmɜːrmər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>低声抱怨，发牢骚</td><td>murmur a complaint<br>低声抱怨一句</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers murmured about the new rules but nobody complained openly.  工人们对新规定低声抱怨，却没人公开抗议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mumble</td><td>/ˈmʌmbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>说话含糊</td><td>mumble an apology<br>含糊地道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He mumbled an apology and hurried out of the crowded room.  他咕哝了一句道歉，匆匆走出拥挤的房间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mutter</td><td>/ˈmʌtər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>低声抱怨</td><td>mutter under one's breath<br>低声嘟囔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He muttered under his breath as he carried the heavy boxes upstairs.  他把沉重的箱子搬上楼时低声嘟囔着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 slur</td><td>/slɜːr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>含糊不清地说；诋毁，污辱</td><td>slur one's words<br>说话含糊不清</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was so tired that he began to slur his words.  他累得开始说话含糊不清。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inarticulate</td><td>/ˌɪnɑrˈtɪkjələt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>表达不清的，说不出话的</td><td>inarticulate with rage<br>气得说不出话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He became inarticulate whenever the topic turned personal.  一旦话题转到私事，他就说不清话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 ineloquent</del></td><td>/ɪnˈɛləkwənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不善言辞的，不流畅的</td><td>an ineloquent reply<br>拙于言辞的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His ineloquent answer disappointed the audience expecting fireworks.  他拙于言辞的回答让期待精彩的听众失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unvocal</del></td><td>/ʌnˈvoʊkəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不发声的，不表态的</td><td>remain unvocal about it<br>对此不表态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The usually loud critics stayed unvocal about the scandal.  平时高声的批评者对这桩丑闻保持沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ascendant</b></td><td>/əˈsendənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有影响力的，主宰的</td><td>in the ascendant<br>处于上升地位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reformers were ascendant in the party for nearly a decade.  改革派在党内近十年处于主宰地位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 superior</td><td>/suːˈpɪriər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>更好的，占优势的；上级</td><td>superior to the rest<br>优于其余各方</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This model is clearly superior to the older version.  这个型号明显优于旧版。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dominant</td><td>/ˈdɑːmɪnənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>处于支配地位的；（基因）显性的</td><td>the dominant player<br>占主导地位的一方</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dominant player in the market set prices that everyone else followed.  市场上占主导地位的那家定的价，其他人都跟着走。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sovereign</td><td>/ˈsɑːvrən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>拥有最高权力的；君主</td><td>a sovereign state<br>主权国家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The island became a sovereign nation after years of struggle.  经过多年抗争，该岛成为主权国家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 having no influence/power</td><td>/ˈhævɪŋ noʊ ˈɪnfluəns ˈpaʊər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有影响力，无权力</td><td>clearly having no influence/power<br>显然毫无影响力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A committee having no influence/power cannot change the policy.  一个毫无权力的委员会无法改变政策。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ascetic</b></td><td>/əˈsetɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>苦行的，禁欲的；苦行者</td><td>an ascetic lifestyle<br>苦修的生活方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The monk led an ascetic life on bread and cold water.  这位僧人以面包和冷水过着自我克制的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abstemious</td><td>/əbˈstiːmiəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（吃喝等）有节制的，节俭的</td><td>an abstemious diet<br>有节制的饮食</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He led an abstemious life, eating little and drinking less.  他生活节制，吃得少喝得更少。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abstinent</td><td>/ˈæbstənənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>节制的，禁欲的</td><td>remain abstinent from alcohol<br>戒酒不饮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed abstinent from meat and wine for ten years.  他十年间不吃肉不喝酒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 self-denying</td><td>/ˌself dɪˈnaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>克己的，自我节制的</td><td>a self-denying habit<br>克己的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His self-denying routine allowed no sweets at all.  他克己的作息完全不允许甜食。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sumptuous</td><td>/ˈsʌmptʃuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>豪华的，奢侈的</td><td>a sumptuous banquet<br>一场豪华的宴会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hotel served a sumptuous banquet for the visiting delegation.  酒店为来访代表团摆了一场奢华的宴会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 luxurious</td><td>/lʌɡˈʒʊriəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>奢侈的</td><td>a luxurious hotel suite<br>豪华的酒店套房</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They spent their anniversary in a luxurious hotel suite overlooking the harbor.  他们在一间俯瞰港口的豪华酒店套房里度过了结婚纪念日。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>aseptic</b></td><td>/ˌeɪˈseptɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>消毒的，无菌的</td><td>aseptic surgical technique<br>无菌手术操作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Surgeons follow strict aseptic procedure before touching any instrument.  外科医生在接触任何器械前都遵守严格的无菌程序。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sterile</td><td>/ˈsterəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的；无菌的</td><td>sterile medical instruments<br>无菌医疗器械</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Surgeons must use sterile instruments to prevent infection during any operation.  外科医生必须使用无菌器械，以防手术中感染。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 germfree</del></td><td>/ˈdʒɜrmfri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无菌的，无病菌的</td><td>a germfree environment<br>无菌环境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lab maintains a germfree environment for breeding these mice.  实验室为饲养这些小鼠保持无菌环境。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 germy</td><td>/ˈdʒɜrmi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沾满细菌的，不洁的</td><td>a germy doorknob<br>布满细菌的门把手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She refused to touch the germy handrail on the crowded subway.  她不愿碰拥挤地铁里那根满是细菌的扶手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unsterile</del></td><td>/ʌnˈstɛrəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未消毒的，有菌的</td><td>unsterile needles and dressings<br>未消毒的针头和敷料</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Using unsterile instruments in surgery can cause a serious infection.  手术中使用未消毒的器械会引起严重感染。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>askew</b></td><td>/əˈskjuː/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>不成直线的（地），歪的（地）</td><td>hang askew on the wall<br>歪斜地挂在墙上</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting hung askew on the wall above the fireplace.  那幅画歪斜地挂在壁炉上方的墙上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crooked</td><td>/ˈkrʊkəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>弯曲的；歪的；不诚实的</td><td>a crooked country road<br>弯曲的乡间小路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old painting hung crooked above the fireplace for years.  那幅旧画歪歪地挂在壁炉上方多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cockeyed</td><td>/ˈkɑːˌkaɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>歪斜的；荒谬的</td><td>a cockeyed scheme<br>荒唐的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He came up with a cockeyed plan to get rich overnight.  他想出一个一夜暴富的荒唐计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 oblique</td><td>/əˈbliːk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>斜的</td><td>an oblique angle<br>斜角</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The roof meets the wall at an oblique angle that complicates repairs.  屋顶与墙以一个斜角相接，使修理变得复杂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lopsided</td><td>/ˌlɑːpˈsaɪdɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不平衡的，不协调的</td><td>a lopsided victory<br>一场悬殊的胜利</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The final score revealed a lopsided victory for the visiting team.  最终比分显示客队取得了一场悬殊的胜利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 skewed</td><td>/skjuːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>偏斜的；有偏差的</td><td>a skewed sample<br>有偏差的样本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The survey gave skewed results because only students answered it.  由于只有学生作答，调查结果出现偏差。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 aslant</del></td><td>/əˈslænt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>副词、介词</td><td>斜地，倾斜地</td><td>lie aslant the road<br>斜卧在路上</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A fallen pine lay aslant the narrow mountain path.  一棵倒下的松树斜横在狭窄的山路上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slanted</td><td>/ˈslæntɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>倾斜的；有偏向的</td><td>slanted news coverage<br>有偏向的新闻报道</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report was clearly slanted in favor of the company.  这份报告明显偏向该公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slanting</td><td>/ˈslæntɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>倾斜的，斜的</td><td>slanting afternoon sunlight<br>倾斜的午后阳光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Slanting rain beat against the windows all through the night.  斜雨整夜拍打着窗户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 listing</td><td>/ˈlɪstɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>（船）倾斜的；清单</td><td>a listing ship<br>倾斜的船</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The listing vessel was towed slowly back into the harbor.  那艘倾斜的船被慢慢拖回港口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tilted</td><td>/ˈtɪltɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>倾斜的，歪的</td><td>a tilted head<br>歪着的头</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She looked at me with her head tilted to one side.  她歪着头看我。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 awry</td><td>/əˈraɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>出差错的（地）</td><td>plans went awry<br>计划出了岔子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 All their careful plans went awry within the first hour.  他们精心的计划在第一个小时内就出了岔子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aligned</td><td>/əˈlaɪnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>对齐的，一致的</td><td>closely aligned interests<br>紧密一致的利益</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two columns are perfectly aligned with the door frame.  两根柱子与门框完全对齐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 6

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>aspect</b></td><td>/ˈæspekt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>外表，容貌；方面</td><td>a stern aspect<br>严厉的神情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old teacher had a stern aspect that frightened new pupils.  这位老教师神情严厉，让新学生害怕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appearance</td><td>/əˈpɪrəns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>外表，外貌；出现</td><td>a neat appearance<br>整洁的外表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not judge a stranger only by his outward appearance.  不要仅凭外表判断一个陌生人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 look</td><td>/ˈlʊk/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>神情，外表；看</td><td>a worried look<br>担忧的神情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was a worried look on the young mother's face.  那位年轻母亲脸上带着担忧的神情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 figure</td><td>/ˈfɪɡjər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>身材，身影；数字；人物</td><td>a slender figure<br>苗条的身材</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A tall figure appeared suddenly at the end of the corridor.  一个高大的身影突然出现在走廊尽头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 presence</td><td>/ˈprezəns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>仪态，风度；在场</td><td>a commanding presence<br>威严的气度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old actor had a stage presence no one could match.  这位老演员有着无人能及的舞台风度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mien</td><td>/ˈmiːn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>风度，仪态，神情</td><td>a grave mien<br>严肃的神情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge entered the courtroom with a solemn and dignified mien.  法官带着庄严的神情走进法庭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>asperity</b></del></td><td>/æˈsperəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（举止、性情）粗暴；（环境）艰苦</td><td>speak with asperity<br>语气尖刻地说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered the reporters with unusual asperity that morning.  那天上午他异常粗暴尖刻地回答了记者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 roughness</td><td>/ˈrʌfnəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>粗糙；粗暴，粗鲁</td><td>the roughness of his manner<br>他举止的粗鲁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The roughness of his reply startled everyone at the table.  他回答的粗鲁让满桌人都吃了一惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crudity</td><td>/ˈkrudɪti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>粗鲁，粗俗；粗糙</td><td>the crudity of his jokes<br>他玩笑的粗俗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crudity of his remarks offended the guests at dinner.  他言辞的粗俗令晚宴上的客人不快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rudeness</td><td>/ˈruːdnəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>粗鲁，无礼</td><td>apologize for one's rudeness<br>为自己的无礼道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her rudeness to the waiter embarrassed everyone in our group.  她对服务员的无礼让我们同行的人都很尴尬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 poignancy</td><td>/ˈpɔɪnjənsi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>辛酸动人；尖刻，深切</td><td>the poignancy of the scene<br>那一幕的动人辛酸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The letters gain poignancy when you learn the writer died young.  得知写信人早逝，这些信便更显辛酸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harshness</td><td>/ˈhɑːrʃnəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>严厉，苛刻；恶劣</td><td>the harshness of the punishment<br>惩罚的严厉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics condemned the harshness of the new immigration rules.  批评者谴责新移民规定的严苛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 severity</td><td>/sɪˈverɪti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>严厉；严重程度</td><td>the severity of the winter<br>冬天的严酷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors were surprised by the severity of her symptoms.  医生对她症状的严重程度感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hardness</td><td>/ˈhɑːrdnəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>坚硬；艰难；冷酷</td><td>the hardness of the stone<br>石头的坚硬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hardness of diamond makes it ideal for cutting tools.  钻石的硬度使它非常适合做切割工具。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hardship</td><td>/ˈhɑːrdʃɪp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>艰难，困苦</td><td>endure great hardship<br>忍受巨大艰苦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The family endured years of hardship after losing their farm.  失去农场后，这家人苦熬了多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rigor</td><td>/ˈrɪɡər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>严格，严厉；艰苦</td><td>the rigors of winter<br>冬天的严酷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students complained about the rigor of the new grading system.  学生抱怨新评分制度过于严格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
